Link Accounts
Benefits of Linking Accounts
If you link your account, you’ll be able to\nrecover your Pokémon Sleep data and continue\nplaying even if you change devices, delete the\napp, or otherwise lose your data.
Also, if you link your account, you can claim\n[System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]100 bonus diamonds[System:Color a="255" ].\nNote: This bonus only applies the first time\nyou link your account.
You can navigate to the Link Accounts screen\nby tapping the Link Accounts button.

This two-week event is a special period where <b>Cresselia shines against Darkrai</b>. During the event, the Legendary Pokémon <strong>Cresselia</strong> is more likely to appear, and a <b>bonus</b> will be applied to <b>Drowsy Power</b> if you have Cresselia on your team when you sleep.\n\nAdditionally, you can obtain samples of <strong>Cresselia Down</strong> during the event through sleep research. You can trade these samples via the Event Exchange for new items—Cresselia Incense and Cresselia Biscuits—to help you discover Cresselia’s sleep styles!\n\nFurthermore, during the event, the average Drowsy Power of researchers worldwide and your own Drowsy Power will be combined to accumulate <strong>Event Drowsy Power</strong>. This Event Drowsy Power may be the key to dispelling Darkrai’s nightmares!\n\nWe encourage you to collaborate with researchers worldwide to dispel Darkrai’s nightmares and rescue the Pokémon!\n\nAnd...if you make good progress during the event, there might be a chance to befriend Darkrai...?\nWe hope you’re looking forward to this!\n\nEvent Period:\n• Week 1: <i data-time="1743393600" data-format="6" data-raw-date="1"></i> to <i data-time="1743998399" data-format="6" data-raw-date="1"></i>\n• Week 2: <i data-time="1743998400" data-format="6" data-raw-date="1"></i> to <i data-time="1744603199" data-format="6" data-raw-date="1"></i>\n\nEvent Areas:\n• All areas\n\nMission Period:\n• Week 1: <i data-time="1743393600" data-format="6" data-raw-date="1"></i> to <i data-time="1744603199" data-format="6" data-raw-date="1"></i>\n• Week 2: <i data-time="1743998400" data-format="6" data-raw-date="1"></i> to <i data-time="1744603199" data-format="6" data-raw-date="1"></i>\n\nEvent Exchange Period:\n• <i data-time="1743393600" data-format="6" data-raw-date="1"></i> to <i data-time="1744775999" data-format="6" data-raw-date="1"></i>\n\nThe following effects will trigger in the applicable areas during the event.\n\n— All Days —\n• You can accumulate limited-time <strong>Event Drowsy Power</strong> and receive rewards based on your event rank.\n• The limited-time <strong>Event Exchange</strong> will be available.\n• You can obtain event items through sleep research once per day.\n• <strong>Certain Pokémon will be more likely to appear</strong> during sleep research.\n• Each time a Psychic-type helper Pokémon brings you ingredients as it regularly would, it will have <strong>one extra</strong>.\n• The chance of any Psychic-type helper Pokémon’s <strong>main skill being triggered will be multiplied by 1.5</strong>.\n• The Snorlax in the event areas will have <strong>Mago Berries</strong> (Psychic type) set as one of its favorite Berries. This two-week event is a special opportunity—\nthe Legendary Pokémon [System:Color r="255" g="212" b="128" a="255" ]Entei [System:Color a="255" ]will appear during\nsleep research for the first time!
During the event, you can collect samples of\n[System:Color r="255" g="212" b="128" a="255" ]Entei Mane[System:Color a="255" ] to trade at the Event Exchange for\nitems, such as [System:Color r="255" g="212" b="128" a="255" ]Entei Incense[System:Color a="255" ] and [System:Color r="255" g="212" b="128" a="255" ]Entei Biscuits[System:Color a="255" ].
You can use Entei Incense to research Entei’s\nvarious sleep styles!
Note: Even without Entei Incense, Entei may\nappear during sleep research—but extremely\nrarely.
For researchers who would like to befriend\nEntei, we recommend using [System:Color r="255" g="212" b="128" a="255" ]Entei Biscuits[System:Color a="255" ],\nwhich are specialized for use with Entei only\nand increase its friendship points by [System:Color r="255" g="212" b="128" a="255" ]6[System:Color a="255" ].
Also, [System:Color r="255" g="212" b="128" a="255" ]Fire-type helper Pokémon[System:Color a="255" ] will be\nparticularly helpful during this event!
In addition, you’ll be able to encounter [System:Color r="255" g="212" b="128" a="255" ]Pokémon\nof different sleep types[System:Color a="255" ] regardless of\nyour own.\n[System:Color r="255" g="212" b="128" a="255" ]Fire-type Pokémon[System:Color a="255" ] in particular will have a\ngreater appearance rate.

We hope you have fun!

Moving to New Sites
Take care, Snorlax! Thanks for a\ngreat week of snoozy studies!
You’ll spend seven days—from Monday\nthrough Sunday—raising a Snorlax each\nweek by building up its Strength as you\ncarry out sleep research. But when the\nweek is over, it will be time to say\ngoodbye to that Snorlax, move to a new\nresearch site, and find a new Snorlax\nto help with your studies.
Don’t worry—you’ll be able to sync\nwith your paired device after you\nmove, and your research will be based\non the site you were in and the status\nof your Snorlax from before you\nmoved.

Fire Type Week
The [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]Flower Festival[System:Color a="255" ] event lasts from 4:00 a.m.\non Monday, April 22, to 3:59 a.m. on Monday,\nApril 29, in all areas!
During this event, you’ll be able to meet\n[System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]Pokémon of various sleep types[System:Color a="255" ] regardless of\nyour own type. In particular, [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]Grass-type and\nFairy-type Pokémon[System:Color a="255" ] will be more likely\nto appear.\nYou may even meet a Shiny Pokémon!
Also during this event, [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]Snorlax’s requested\ndishes will be set to “Salads,” and the final\nStrength value for dishes will be multiplied\nby 1.5[System:Color a="255" ].\nNote: The multiplier for Extra Tasty dishes will\nbe different depending on the day. On Sunday,\nExtra Tasty dishes will give 4.5 times as much\nStrength, and on the other days, it will give\n3 times as much.
And during this week-long event, [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]one of the\nPokémon[System:Color a="255" ] that visits during sleep research will\n[System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]for sure[System:Color a="255" ] arrive [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]hungry[System:Color a="255" ]!\nNotes:\nThis applies to only one sleep session\nper day.\nThis effect applies separately from the hungry\neffects of Friend Incense and Good Camp\nTickets.\nExample: If you use a Good Camp Ticket and\nFriend Incense together during the event, at\nleast three of the Pokémon that show up during\nsleep research will for sure be hungry.
In addition, through [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]limited-time missions[System:Color a="255" ]\nduring the event, you can get great rewards,\nsuch as [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]Comfey Incense[System:Color a="255" ].
We hope you’re looking forward to it!
● How to Progress in the Event\nParticipating in the event is easy!\nSimply tap the Sleep button before going to\nsleep during the event period to track\nyour sleep.\nA variety of event-related effects will trigger\nduring your sleep research.\nNote: Event bonuses can trigger in any area\nduring this event.
● Bonuses\n— Last Day of the Event (4/28) —\n• During sleep research, [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]one Pokémon will for\nsure be hungry[System:Color a="255" ].\n• Dishes will be set to [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]“Salads.”[System:Color a="255" ]\n• Final Strength gains from dishes will be\nmultiplied by [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]1.5 [System:Color a="255" ](or 4.5 for Extra Tasty\ndishes).\n• [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]A few Pokémon of different sleep types will\nappear[System:Color a="255" ] during sleep research regardless of your\nsleep type for that day.\n• [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]Certain Pokémon will be more likely to appear\n[System:Color a="255" ]during sleep research.\n• You may meet Shiny Pokémon.
— Other Days (4/22–4/27) —\n• During sleep research, [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]one Pokémon will for\nsure be hungry[System:Color a="255" ].\n• Dishes will be set to [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]“Salads.”[System:Color a="255" ]\n• Final Strength gains from dishes will be\nmultiplied by [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]1.5[System:Color a="255" ] (or 3 for Extra Tasty dishes).\n• [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]A few Pokémon of different sleep types will\nappear[System:Color a="255" ] during sleep research regardless of your\nsleep type for that day.\n• [System:Color r="255" g="244" b="147" a="255" ]Certain Pokémon will be more likely to appear[System:Color a="255" ]\nduring sleep research.\n• You may meet Shiny Pokémon.
● Pokémon that are easier to encounter during\nthe event\nDuring the event, the following Pokémon are\nmore likely to appear!
Greater Appearance Rate\n(Grass types)\n• Bulbasaur, Ivysaur, Venusaur, Bellsprout,\nWeepinbell, Victreebel, Chikorita, Bayleef,\nMeganium, Leafeon
(Fairy types)\n• Clefairy, Clefable, Jigglypuff, Wigglytuff,\nCleffa, Igglybuff, Togepi, Togetic, Togekiss,\nSylveon, Comfey
For more details about the event, please see\nthe in-game News.
Flower Festival
Rest your very best on the night of the full moon!\nThis event is held once a month for a total of three days—on the day of the full moon and the days before and after it—and provides a boost to Drowsy Power!\nLet’s try to get lots of good sleep during this time and research loads of Pokémon sleep styles!\n\nThe event also includes bonus sleep points and bonuses to Pokémon’s Sleep EXP gains.\n\nWe hope you’re looking forward to the event!\n\nEvent Period:\n• Monday, August 19, at 4:00 a.m. to Thursday, August 22, at 3:59 a.m.\nNote: <strong>The day of the full moon is Tuesday, August 20.</strong>\n\nEvent Areas:\n• All areas\n\nThe following bonuses will trigger when you conduct sleep research in applicable areas during the event.\n\n— Night of the Full Moon (August 20) —\nDrowsy Power <strong>×3</strong>\nHelper Pokémon Sleep EXP ×3\nBonus Sleep Points +1,000\n\n— Other Days (August 19 and 21) —\nDrowsy Power <strong>×1.5</strong>\nHelper Pokémon Sleep EXP ×2\nBonus Sleep Points +500

Good Sleep Day
Trick or treat!\n\nThis week-long event is a special time when <strong>Ghost-type Pokémon</strong> are more likely to appear!\nDuring this event, you may even meet a <strong>Pikachu wearing a Halloween hat</strong>.\n\nYou can get fun rewards—such as <strong>Handy Candy S</strong>—from <strong>limited-time missions</strong>.\n\nYou can also get <strong>at least 2 times the usual number of candies</strong> from sleep research. Try getting lots of candies during this event!\n\nAnd during this event only, <strong>Gengar</strong>, <strong>Drifblim</strong>, and <strong>Skeledirge</strong> can appear on <b>Greengrass Isle</b>!\n\nThere will also be other bonuses.\nWe hope you’re looking forward to this event!\n\nEvent Period:\n• Monday, October 28, at 4:00 a.m. to Monday, November 4, 3:59 a.m.\n\nEvent Area:\n• Greengrass Isle\n\nMission Period:\n• Monday, October 28, at 4:00 a.m. to Monday, November 4, 3:59 a.m.\n\nNote: The following effects will trigger in the applicable areas during this event.\n\n— All Days (10/28 to 11/3) —\n• <strong>Certain Pokémon will be more likely to appear</strong> during sleep research.\n• Each time a Ghost-type helper Pokémon brings you ingredients during regular helping, <strong>it will have one extra</strong>.\n• The chance of a Ghost-type helper Pokémon’s <strong>main skill being triggered will be multiplied by 1.5</strong>.\n• <strong>Main skill levels</strong> of Ghost-type helper Pokémon will be <strong>boosted by 1</strong>.\n• The Snorlax in the event area will have <strong>Bluk Berries</strong> (Ghost type) set as one of its favorite Berries. The other two kinds of Berries are chosen at random.\n• <strong>Many Pokémon of different sleep types will appear</strong> during sleep research regardless of the researcher’s sleep type for that day.\n\n— Halloween (10/31) and the Last Day of the Event (11/3) —\n• Researchers will gain <strong>3 times the usual number of candies</strong> for the first sleep research of the day.\n\n— Other Days (10/28 to 10/30 and 11/1 to 11/2) —\n• Researchers will gain <strong>2 times the usual number of candies</strong> for the first sleep research of the day.
Halloween Event
Beginning at 4:00 a.m. on Monday, October 30,\nwe will be holding an event:\n[System:Color r="255" g="185" b="47" a="255" ]Halloween 2023 — Double Candy Research[System:Color a="255" ]!
Trick or treat!\nDuring the week-long event, “Halloween 2023 —\nDouble Candy Research,” you’ll be able to\nencounter Pokémon of various sleep types,\nregardless of your own sleep type.\nAlso, you can get lavish rewards, such as a total\nof [System:Color r="255" g="185" b="47" a="255" ]Handy Candy S ×25[System:Color a="255" ] through special missions\nthat will be added to the usual missions, and\nyou can get [System:Color r="255" g="185" b="47" a="255" ]at least [Digit:Digit2 ] times the usual number\nof Pokémon candies[System:Color a="255" ] from sleep research.\nTry to get lots of candies during this event!\nAnd this time you’ll have lots of opportunities\nto meet [System:Color r="255" g="185" b="47" a="255" ]Pikachu wearing a Halloween hat[System:Color a="255" ] and\nalso [System:Color r="255" g="185" b="47" a="255" ]Ghost-type Pokémon such as Gastly[System:Color a="255" ]!\nWe hope you’re looking forward to it!
• How to Progress in the Event\nParticipating in the event is easy!\nSimply tap the “Sleep” button on\n[System:Color r="255" g="185" b="47" a="255" ]Greengrass Isle[System:Color a="255" ] before going to sleep during\nthe event period to track your sleep.\nA variety of event-related effects will be\ntriggered during your sleep research.\nNote: Tracking sleep on any map other than\nGreengrass Isle will not trigger the event\nbonuses. The effects described below will apply\nduring the event period.

Halloween Event
Happy holidays!\n\nThis week-long event is a special time when <strong>Ice-type Pokémon</strong> are particularly active!\n\nDuring this event, certain Pokémon—such as <strong>Vulpix (Alolan Form)</strong> and <strong>Snover</strong>—will appear on <strong>Greengrass Isle</strong>!\n\nYou may even meet <strong>Eevee or Pikachu wearing a red hat</strong> or a Shiny Pokémon!\n\nThere will be various other bonuses as well, including opportunities to get <strong>double or more Dream Shards</strong> during sleep research and use the <strong>Candy Boost</strong> function.\n\nAlso, <strong>Dream Gifts</strong> will be delivered to Snorlax’s side on Tuesday, December 24, and Wednesday, December 25.\n\nWe hope you’re looking forward to this!\n\nEvent Period:\n• Monday, December 23, at 4:00 a.m. to Monday, December 30, at 3:59 a.m.\n\nEvent Area:\n• Greengrass Isle\n\nMission Period:\n• Monday, December 23, at 4:00 a.m. to Monday, December 30, at 3:59 a.m.\n\nThe following effects will trigger in the applicable areas during this event.\n\n— All Days (December 23 to 29) —\n• <strong>Certain Pokémon will be more likely to appear</strong> during sleep research.\n• <strong>Main skill Dream Shard Magnet S will be twice as effective.</strong>\n• You may meet a Shiny Pokémon.\n• The limited-time <strong>Candy Boost</strong> function will be available.\n• The Snorlax in the event area will have <strong>Rawst Berries</strong> (Ice type) set as one of its favorite Berries. The other two kinds of Berries are chosen at random.\n• Each time an Ice-type helper Pokémon brings you ingredients during regular helping, <strong>it will have one extra</strong>.\n• The chance of an Ice-type helper Pokémon’s <strong>main skill being triggered will be multiplied by 1.5</strong>.\n• <strong>Main skill levels</strong> of Ice-type helper Pokémon will be <strong>boosted by 1</strong>.\n• <strong>Pokémon of different sleep types will appear</strong> during sleep research regardless of your sleep type for that day.\n\n— December 24 —\n• You can receive a limited-time <strong>Dream Gift</strong>.\n• Researchers will gain <strong>twice the usual number of Dream Shards</strong> through sleep research.\n\n— December 25 —\n• You can receive a limited-time <strong>Dream Gift</strong>.\n• Researchers will gain <strong>three times the usual number of Dream Shards</strong> through sleep research.\n• Researchers will receive <strong>1,225 bonus sleep points</strong>.\n\n— Last Day of the Event (December 29) —\n• Researchers will gain <strong>three times the usual number of Dream Shards</strong> through sleep research.\n\n— Other Days (December 23 and 26–28) —\n• Researchers will gain <strong>twice the usual number of Dream Shards</strong> through sleep research.
An event on [System:Color r="105" g="211" b="255" a="255" ]Greengrass Isle[System:Color a="255" ] will last from\n4:00 a.m. on Monday, December 18:\n[System:Color r="105" g="211" b="255" a="255" ]Holiday 2023 — Double Dream Shard Research[System:Color a="255" ]!\nHoliday 2023 — Double Dream Shard Research is\na week-long event during which you can get\n[System:Color r="105" g="211" b="255" a="255" ]double the Dream Shards[System:Color a="255" ] through sleep\nresearch, and you may even meet a Shiny\nPokémon!\nThere will also be an event-limited function\ncalled [System:Color r="105" g="211" b="255" a="255" ]Candy Boost[System:Color a="255" ] that will double the\neffectiveness of candies!\nThe main skill [System:Color r="105" g="211" b="255" a="255" ]Dream Shard Magnet S will be\ntwice as effective[System:Color a="255" ] as well during the event,\nso this is an opportunity to really raise up your\nPokémon!
In addition, the Snorlax on Greengrass Isle\nduring the event week will have [System:Color r="105" g="211" b="255" a="255" ]Rawst Berries[System:Color a="255" ]\n(Ice type) set as one of its favorite Berries.\nAnd for this event as well, you’ll be able to\nmeet Pokémon of [System:Color r="105" g="211" b="255" a="255" ]different sleep types[System:Color a="255" ]\nregardless of your own!\nThrough[System:Color r="105" g="211" b="255" a="255" ] limited-time missions[System:Color a="255" ] during the\nevent, you can get great rewards such as up to\n[System:Color r="105" g="211" b="255" a="255" ]a total of 60 Pikachu candies[System:Color a="255" ].\nAlso, researchers who conduct sleep research\non the final day of the event will get a gift of\n[System:Color r="105" g="211" b="255" a="255" ]1,225 bonus sleep points[System:Color a="255" ]!\nOn top of that, you’ll have a higher likelihood\nof meeting [System:Color r="105" g="211" b="255" a="255" ]Pokémon such as Delibird, Snover,\nand Abomasnow[System:Color a="255" ], as well as [System:Color r="105" g="211" b="255" a="255" ]a Pikachu wearing a\nred hat[System:Color a="255" ]!\nWe hope you’re looking forward to the event!

New Year Event
This two week–long event is a special time when <strong>Pokémon who arrived in Pokémon Sleep in 2024</strong> are more likely to appear.\n\nDuring this event, certain Pokémon—such as <strong>Dratini</strong> and <strong>Aron</strong>—will appear on <strong>Greengrass Isle</strong>!\n\nAlso, the <strong>Event Exchange</strong>—where you can trade <strong>Dream Coins</strong>—for various items will be available.\n\nAdditionally, you’ll be able to use the <strong>Candy Cram-o-matic</strong> function during the second week of the event!\n\nWe plan to have different bonuses and Pokémon with greater appearance rates for the first and second week of this event.\n\nIt’s easy to let our sleep become inconsistent this time of year, so we hope you and your helper Pokémon can start off your year with good, consistent sleep!\nWe hope you’re looking forward to the event!\n\nEvent Period:\n• Week 1: Monday, December 30, at 4:00 a.m. to Monday, January 6, at 3:59 a.m.\n• Week 2: Monday, January 6, at 4:00 a.m. to Monday, January 13, at 3:59 a.m.\n\nEvent Areas:\n• All areas\n\nMission Period:\n• Week 1: Monday, December 30, at 4:00 a.m. to Monday, January 13, at 3:59 a.m.\n• Week 2: Monday, January 6, at 4:00 a.m. to Monday, January 13, at 3:59 a.m.\n\nEvent Exchange Period:\n• Monday, December 30, at 4:00 a.m. to Wednesday, January 15, at 3:59 a.m.\n\nThe following effects will trigger in the applicable areas during the event.\n\n— All Days (December 30 to January 12) —\n• <strong>Pokémon of different sleep types will appear</strong> during sleep research regardless of your sleep type for that day.\n• <strong>Certain Pokémon will be more likely to appear</strong> during sleep research.\n• The limited-time <strong>Event Exchange</strong> will be available.\n\n— Week 1 (December 30 to January 5) —\n• <strong>Candies gained</strong> from the first sleep research of the day will be <strong>multiplied by 1.5</strong>.\n• The chance of each helper Pokémon’s <strong>main skill triggering</strong> will be <strong>multiplied by 1.25</strong>.\n• <strong>Main skill levels</strong> of helper Pokémon will be <strong>boosted by 1</strong>.\n\n— Week 2 (January 6 to January 12) — \n• Researchers will gain <strong>1.5 times the usual number of Dream Shards</strong> through sleep research.\n• Final Strength gains from dishes will be <strong>multiplied by 1.25</strong> (or <b>2.5 for Extra Tasty dishes or 3.75 for Extra Tasty dishes on Sunday only</b>).\n• The limited-time function <strong>Candy Cram-o-matic</strong> will be available.\n\n— January 1 —\n• Drowsy Power <strong>×3</strong>.\n\n— January 2 to January 3 —\n• Drowsy Power <strong>×1.5</strong>.

The [System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Raikou Research[System:Color a="255" ] event lasts from 4:00 a.m.\non Monday, March 25, to 3:59 a.m. on Monday,\nApril 8, on [System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Greengrass Isle[System:Color a="255" ]!
This two-week event is a special opportunity—\nthe Legendary Pokémon [System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Raikou[System:Color a="255" ] will appear\nduring sleep research for the first time!
During the event, you can collect samples of\n[System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Raikou Mane[System:Color a="255" ] to trade at the Event Exchange\nfor items, such as [System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Raikou Incense[System:Color a="255" ] and\n[System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Raikou Biscuits[System:Color a="255" ].
You can use Raikou Incense to research Raikou’s\nvarious sleep styles!
Note: Even without Raikou Incense, Raikou may\nappear during sleep research—but extremely\nrarely.
For researchers who would like to befriend\nRaikou, we recommend using [System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Raikou Biscuits[System:Color a="255" ],\nwhich are specialized for use with Raikou only\nand increase its friendship points by [System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]6[System:Color a="255" ].
Also, [System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Electric-type helper Pokémon[System:Color a="255" ] will be\nparticularly helpful during this event!
In addition, you’ll be able to encounter\n[System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Pokémon of different sleep types[System:Color a="255" ] regardless of\nyour own.\n[System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Electric-type Pokémon[System:Color a="255" ] in particular will have a\ngreater appearance rate.
During the event, you’ll be able to take on two\nsets of limited-time missions, [System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Week 1 Missions[System:Color a="255" ]\nand [System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Week 2 Missions[System:Color a="255" ], to earn samples of\n[System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Raikou Mane[System:Color a="255" ] as rewards!
Week 1 Mission period:\nMonday, March 25, at 4:00 a.m. to\nMonday, April 8, at 3:59 a.m.\nWeek 2 Mission period:\nMonday, April 1, at 4:00 a.m. to\nMonday, April 8, at 3:59 a.m.
We hope you have fun!
● Event Exchange\nExchange Period:\n[System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Monday, March 25, at 4:00 a.m. to\nThursday, April 11, at 3:59 a.m.[System:Color a="255" ]
This is a shop where you can exchange samples\nof Raikou Mane for the following items.\n• Raikou Incense\n• Raikou Biscuit\n• Main Skill Seed\n• Thunder Stone\nAnd more!
● Raikou Mane\n• During the event, Raikou will leave behind bits\nof its mane, which you can collect as samples.\n• You can check to see how many samples you\nhave at the Event Exchange (the samples won’t\ngo into your bag’s items pocket).\n• After the event ends, you can still trade\nsamples of Raikou Mane until the Event\nExchange closes. Once the exchange period\nends, any remaining samples of Raikou Mane\nwill be lost.
● How to Get Samples of Raikou Mane\nThe main ways to collect samples of\nRaikou Mane are listed below.
[System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]■ Sleep research on Greengrass Isle\n[System:Color a="255" ]• You can get them during one sleep research\nsession per day.\nNote: You cannot get any during the second\nsleep research of the day.
[System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]■ Limited-time missions\n[System:Color a="255" ]• You can get samples of Raikou Mane by\ncompleting limited-time missions.
[System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]■ Raikou Research Bundles Vol. 1 and Raikou\nLast Chance Bundles Vol. 1\n[System:Color a="255" ]Please see in-game news about the bundles for\ndetails.
● How to Progress in the Event\nParticipating in the event is easy!\nSimply press the “Sleep” button before going to\nsleep during the event to track your sleep.\nA variety of event-related effects will trigger\nduring your sleep research.\nNote: This event’s bonuses apply only on\n[System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Greengrass Isle[System:Color a="255" ].\nThe effects described below will apply during\nthe event period.
— All Days (3/25 to 4/7) —\n• Each time an Electric-type Pokémon brings\nyou ingredients, it will have one extra\ningredient.\n• The chance of an Electric-type helper\nPokémon’s main skill being triggered will be\nmultiplied by 1.5.\n• The Snorlax on Greengrass Isle will have\n[System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Grepa Berries[System:Color a="255" ] (Electric type) set as one of its\nfavorite Berries.\n• [System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]A few Pokémon of different sleep types will\nappear[System:Color a="255" ] during sleep research regardless of the\nresearcher’s sleep type for that day.\n• [System:Color r="255" g="215" b="72" a="255" ]Certain Pokémon will be more likely to appear[System:Color a="255" ]\nduring sleep research.
● Pokémon that are easier to encounter during\nthe event\nDuring the event, the following Pokémon will be\nmore likely to appear!
Slightly Greater Appearance Rate\nPikachu, Raichu, Jolteon, Pichu, Mareep,\nFlaaffy, Raikou, Dedenne
For more details about the event, please see the\nin-game News.

It’s time to celebrate Pokémon Sleep’s second anniversary!
This two-week event is a special time to celebrate the second anniversary of Pokémon Sleep’s global release on [News:Date_mda ].
In addition to the arrival of Pokémon from the Hoenn region—such as [News:PokemonName idx="1" ], [News:PokemonName idx="2" ], and [News:PokemonName idx="3" ]—we’re also planning to add a new <strong>photo mode</strong> feature.
And it’s not just Treecko, [News:PokemonName idx="1" ], and [News:PokemonName idx="2" ]! Hoenn’s own <strong>[News:PokemonName idx="3" ]</strong> and <strong>[News:PokemonName idx="4" ]</strong> will be arriving, too!
Also, <b>limited-time</b> <strong>Dream Gifts</strong> will <b>appear each day next to Snorlax</b> during the first week of this event.
You’ll see what’s inside the Dream Gifts when you open them!
During the second week, there will be a <b>new limited-time feature</b> that gives various research bonuses, plus there will be <strong>various other bonuses</strong>, such as the opportunity to use <strong>Mini Candy Boost</strong>.
On the day of the second anniversary, <b>[News:Date_mda idx="1" ]</b>, we’ll also be giving item gifts—including <strong>Pokémon incenses</strong> for [News:PokemonName idx="2" ] and [News:PokemonName idx="3" ]—to help you <b>befriend and raise these Pokémon</b>!
On top of all that, you can complete <strong>limited-time missions</strong> the first week to get rewards such as <strong>Pokémon incense</strong> for [News:PokemonName idx="1" ], [News:PokemonName idx="2" ], and [News:PokemonName idx="3" ], and <strong>Pokémon candies</strong> for the second week’s missions.
We hope you’re looking forward to this!
Event Periods:
• Week 1: [News:Date_ymdhm idx="1" ] to [News:Date_ymdhm idx="2" ]
• Week 2: [News:Date_ymdhm idx="1" ] to [News:Date_ymdhm idx="2" ]
Event Areas:
• Week 1: All areas
• Week 2: All areas
Mission Periods:
• Week 1 Missions: [News:Date_ymdhm idx="1" ] to [News:Date_ymdhm idx="2" ]
• Week 2 Missions: [News:Date_ymdhm idx="1" ] to [News:Date_ymdhm idx="2" ]
The following effects will trigger in the applicable areas during this event.
— All Days —
• <strong>A few Pokémon of different sleep types will appear</strong> during sleep research regardless of your sleep type for that day.
• <strong>Certain Pokémon will be more likely to appear</strong> during sleep research.
— Week 1 ([News:Date_md idx="1" ] to [News:Date_md idx="2" ]) — A week when you can befriend lots of helper Pokémon!
• During sleep research, <strong>one Pokémon will for sure arrive hungry</strong> (once per day).
• The chance of each helper Pokémon’s <strong>main skill triggering</strong> will be <strong>multiplied by [News:Num_Count ]</strong>.
• Helper Pokémon Sleep EXP <strong>×[News:Num_Count ]</strong>.
• The <strong>number of candies gained</strong> through sleep research will be <strong>multiplied by [News:Num_Count ]</strong> (once per day).
• A limited-time <strong>Dream Gift</strong> can be received each day.
— Week 2 ([News:Date_md idx="1" ] to [News:Date_md idx="2" ]) — A week when you can raise lots of helper Pokémon!
• Final Strength gains from dishes will be multiplied by <strong>1.5</strong> (<b>or 3 for Extra Tasty dishes or 4.5 for Extra Tasty dishes on Sunday only</b>).
• The limited-time <strong>Mini Candy Boost</strong> function will be available.
• A new limited-time feature that gives various research bonuses will be available. Details to be announced at a later date.
It’s time to celebrate Pokémon Sleep’s third anniversary!
This two-week event is a special time to celebrate the third anniversary of Pokémon Sleep’s global release on [News:Date_mda ].
You can now encounter Pokémon from the Sinnoh region—such as [News:PokemonNamesList Type="And" ]!
Also, <b>limited-time</b> <strong>Dream Gifts</strong> will <b>appear each day next to Snorlax</b> during the first week of this event.
You’ll see what’s inside the Dream Gifts when you open them!
During the second week, there will be <strong>Festive Flowers</strong> and <strong>Mini Candy Boost</strong>, which give various research bonuses. There will also be Neutralizing Mints—plus a new feature that we plan to make fully available outside of the event period.
On the day of the third anniversary, <b>[News:Date_mda ]</b>, we’ll be giving a gift to celebrate and express our appreciation!
On top of all that, you can complete <strong>limited-time missions</strong> during the first week to get rewards such as <strong>Pokémon incense</strong> for [News:PokemonNamesList ], and <strong>Pokémon candies</strong> for the second week’s missions.
We hope you’re looking forward to this!

Rest your very best on the night of the full moon!
This event is held once a month for a total of three days—on the day of the full moon and the days before and after it—and provides a boost to Drowsy Power!
Let’s try to get lots of good sleep during this time and research loads of Pokémon sleep styles!
The event also includes bonus sleep points and bonuses to Pokémon’s Sleep EXP gains.
We hope you’re looking forward to this!

Let’s try to get lots of good sleep during this time and research loads of Pokémon sleep styles!
The event also includes bonus sleep points and bonuses to Pokémon’s Sleep EXP gains.
We hope you’re looking forward to this!
Event Period:
• [News:Date_mdahm idx="1" ] to [News:Date_mdahm idx="2" ]
Note: The day of the full moon is <strong>[News:Date_mda ]</strong>.
Event Areas:
• All areas
The following effects will trigger in the applicable areas during this event.
— All Days —
• <strong>Pokémon sleep styles you haven’t discovered yet are more likely to appear</strong> during sleep research.
— Day 2 (Night of the Full Moon) —
• Drowsy Power <strong>×[News:Num_Count ]</strong>
• Helper Pokémon Sleep EXP <strong>×[News:Num_Count ]</strong>
• Bonus sleep points <strong>+[News:Num_Count ]</strong>
— Day 1 and Day 3 —
• Drowsy Power <strong>×[News:Num_Count ]</strong>
• Helper Pokémon Sleep EXP <strong>×[News:Num_Count ]</strong>
• Bonus sleep points <strong>+[News:Num_Count ]</strong>

Trick or treat!
This week-long event is a special time when <strong>Ghost-type Pokémon</strong> are more likely to appear!
During this event, you may even meet the new arrivals, <strong>[News:PokemonName idx="1" ] and [News:PokemonName idx="2" ], and an [News:PokemonName idx="3" ] wearing a Halloween hat</strong>.
You can get fun rewards from <strong>limited-time missions</strong>, such as <strong>Handy Candies S</strong> and a <strong>Linking Cord</strong> to evolve Pumpkaboo.
You can also get <strong>at least 2 times the usual number of candies</strong> from sleep research.
Try getting lots of candies during this event!
And during this event, Ghost-type Pokémon such as <strong>[News:PokemonName ]</strong> can appear on Greengrass Isle and Greengrass Isle (Expert)!
To commemorate this event, new recipes using <strong>a new ingredient, Plump Pumpkin</strong>, will be added! <strong>One new Dessert/Drink recipe</strong> and <strong>one new Curry/Stew recipe</strong> will be added on October 27, 2025, at 4:00 a.m.
There will also be other bonuses.
We hope you’re looking forward to this!

• [News:Date_mdahm idx="1" ] to [News:Date_mdahm idx="2" ]

— All Days —
• <strong>Certain Pokémon will be more likely to appear</strong> during sleep research.
• Each time a Ghost-type helper Pokémon brings you ingredients as it regularly would, it will have <strong>[News:Num_Count ] extra</strong>.
• The chance of any Ghost-type helper Pokémon’s <strong>main skill being triggered will be multiplied by [News:Num_Count ]</strong>.
• <strong>Main skill levels</strong> of Ghost-type helper Pokémon will be <strong>boosted by [News:Num_Count ]</strong> when they trigger.
• The Snorlax in the event areas will have <strong>Bluk Berries</strong> (Ghost type) set as one of its favorite Berries. The other two kinds of Berries are chosen at random.
• <strong>A few Pokémon of different sleep types will appear</strong> during sleep research regardless of your sleep type for that day.
— Halloween ([News:Date_md idx="1" ]) and the Last Day of the Event ([News:Date_md idx="2" ]) —
• <strong>Candies gained</strong> from the first sleep research of the day will be <strong>multiplied by [News:Num_Count ]</strong>.
— Other Days ([News:Date_md idx="1" ] to [News:Date_md idx="2" ] and [News:Date_md idx="3" ]) —
• <strong>Candies gained</strong> from the first sleep research of the day will be <strong>multiplied by [News:Num_Count ]</strong>.
Happy holidays!
This week-long event is a special time when <strong>Ice-type Pokémon</strong> are particularly active!
And to commemorate <strong>[News:PokemonName ]</strong>’s debut, <strong>Steel-type Pokémon</strong> will be particularly active, too!
Also during this event, certain Pokémon—such as <strong>[News:PokemonName ]</strong>—will appear on <strong>Greengrass Isle</strong>!
You may even meet <strong>[News:PokemonName ] wearing a holiday wreath</strong> or a Shiny Pokémon!
There will be various other bonuses as well, including opportunities to get <strong>double or more Dream Shards</strong> during sleep research and use the <strong>Candy Boost</strong> function.
Additionally, <strong>Dream Gifts</strong> will be delivered to Snorlax’s side on [News:Date_mda idx="1" ] and [News:Date_mda idx="2" ].
We hope you’re looking forward to this!

• Drowsy Power <strong>×[News:Num_Count ]</strong>
This two-week event is a special period where the combined strength of <strong>[News:PokemonName ] and [News:PokemonName idx="1" ]</strong> shine!
During the event, Legendary Pokémon <strong>[News:PokemonNamesList Type="And" ]</strong> are more likely to appear, and a <b>bonus</b> will be applied to <b>Drowsy Power</b> if you have [News:PokemonNamesList idx="1" Type="Or" ] on your team when you sleep.
<b>A larger bonus</b> will be applied to <b>Drowsy Power</b> if you have both Pokémon on your team when you sleep.
You’ll also only be able to get <strong>Latios Down</strong> from sleep research while the event is going on.
You can trade these Latios Down samples via the Event Exchange for new items—Latios Incense, Latias and Latios Biscuits, and Latias Incense—to help you see the sleep styles of [News:PokemonName ] and [News:PokemonName idx="1" ]!
Furthermore, during the event, the average Drowsy Power of researchers worldwide and your own Drowsy Power will be combined to accumulate <strong>Event Drowsy Power</strong>.
This Event Drowsy Power may be the key to restoring the Soul Dew’s brilliance!
We encourage you to collaborate with researchers worldwide to restore the Soul Dew’s brilliance!
And...it seems [News:PokemonName ] and [News:PokemonName idx="1" ] haven’t unlocked their true power yet.
If you make good progress during the event, there might be a chance to unlock the power of the bond between the two.
We hope you’re looking forward to this!

• <strong>A few Pokémon of different sleep types will appear</strong> during sleep research regardless of your sleep type for that day.

In addition to being able to use the limited-time <strong>Candy Cram-o-matic</strong> feature, <strong>Water-, Flying-, and Bug-type Pokémon</strong> are more likely to appear!
We hope you’re looking forward to this!

Text File : MD_fields

The largest island in the region, known for\nits fertile land covered with greenery.\nIt hosts the greatest variety of Pokémon.
This shore of fine white sand stretches to\nthe horizon, kissed by a gentle sea breeze.\nMany Water-type Pokémon live here.
Shafts of sunlight pour through the open\nceiling to light up the cavern walls. Rock-\nand Ground-type Pokémon love it here.
Gorgeous flowers bloom in this freezing\ntundra, despite the unrelenting snowfall.\nMany Ice-type Pokémon can be found here.
Retired long ago, this vast power plant\nstill hosts a lot of abandoned equipment—\nas well as many Electric-type Pokémon.
The scant plant life growing in patches\namid the flowing magma is very tenacious.\nFire-type Pokémon are abundant here.
A thin mist hangs over this eerie lakeside,\nwhere the only sound is the gentle lapping\nof waves. Dragon-type Pokémon rule here.
This remote canyon’s harsh environment has\nnot deterred plants from adapting.\nDragon- and Bug-type Pokémon are abundant.
Sunlight flickers through the foliage.\nThe greenery grows even deeper beyond,\ninspiring adventurous anticipation.
The waves beating against the beach and the\ngentle light shining in is very calming. The\nendless shore may bring new discoveries.

The buds that ring Bayleef’s neck give off a spicy\naroma that perks people up. Maybe the Pokémon\nsleeping around Bayleef feel invigorated, too.
The fragrance given off by its flower soothes and\ncalms emotions. Fewer Pokémon fight around\nMeganium—you’ll often even see them sleeping\npeacefully side-by-side.
Apparently, it often sleeps curled up. On very rare\noccasions, a sleep-befuddled Cyndaquil will spout\nflames from its back while sleeping.
On very rare occasions, you’ll see the flames on its\nback blaze brightly while it sleeps. It seems that no\nmatter how high the flames burn, the fur covering\nQuilava’s body absolutely never catches fire.
It’s said that when Typhlosion tosses and turns in\nits sleep, its fur rubs together and occasionally\nproduces blasts of fire. Take care not to venture\nnear a sleeping Typhlosion by accident!
This Pokémon is known to close one eye and rest\njust half its brain at a time, a practice called\nunihemispheric sleep. Perhaps it’s keeping watch\nagainst an enemy attack.
It’s known to rest one brain hemisphere at a time,\nwith the eye on the opposite side closed. Perhaps\nthis method of sleeping with alternating halves of\nits brain is a defense against enemy attacks.
Feraligatr, too, is known to sleep by resting one\nbrain hemisphere at a time. Be warned—it always\nkeeps a vigilant eye on its surroundings and will\nrush at enemies as soon as it senses their presence.
If you listen carefully while it’s sleeping, you can\nhear the crackle of static electricity. Apparently,\nit sometimes discharges energy in its sleep—maybe\nbecause it’s not yet skilled at storing electricity.
Its body sways as it sleeps, as if rocked in a cradle.\nIts swaying silhouette looks just like a star that\ntwinkles in the night sky.
Its soft and elastic body makes it bounce even in\nits sleep. On very rare occasions, it apparently\npractices its singing in its dreams.
It rolls around in its shell as it sleeps. Legend has\nit that happiness will come to those who can\nsuccessfully stand up a sleeping Togepi.
It sometimes sleeps while floating and apparently\nonly moves its wings intermittently while doing so.\nPerhaps some sort of mysterious power is keeping it\nin the air.
This Pokémon cannot fly because its wings are\nnot yet fully grown, but it can move by hopping\nwith its outstanding jumping power. It can be\nseen stretching out its legs while sleeping.\nThis closely resembles the way it moves when it\nhops onto tree branches.
Xatu stands motionless while staring at the sun.\nIt’s thought it does this to see into the future.\nHowever, when it’s sleeping and broadly flapping\nits wings, it probably isn’t seeing the future.
When Mareep rolls over in its sleep, its fluffy coat\nof wool rubs together and builds a static charge.\nThis apparently turns into an electric current that\nflows into Mareep’s tail, sometimes causing it to\nheat rapidly and start glowing at the tip.

Wooper usually lives in water and has a habit of\nburying half its body in the mud at the bottom of\nthe water to sleep. When it sleeps on land, it\nburies itself in mud to keep its skin from\ndehydrating.
Quagsire has a carefree personality, and it hunts for\nfood by leaving its mouth wide open in water and\nwaiting for its prey to blunder in unaware. If you see\nit asleep with its mouth wide open, maybe it’s\nwaiting for prey, even in its dreams.
It bathes in the sunshine during the day and builds\nup psychic power in the orb on its forehead. It’s said\nto be averse to battling at night, so it might be\neasier than you’d expect to come across an Espeon\ndeep in sleep.
When exposed to the moon’s aura at night, the rings\non its body glow. It’s said to be nocturnal, so it\nmight be very difficult to find an Umbreon sleeping\nat night.

It desperately tries to keep its black tail hidden.\nThough it keeps up the effort even while asleep,\nit’s said that when Wobbuffet sleeps deeply and\ndrops its guard, its tail can be seen on full display.
Even when Steelix is nodding off and looks ready to\ntopple over, it somehow keeps its huge body upright.\nResearchers observed it for an entire night, but it\nheld out with superb balance and didn’t topple over\neven once.

A talented magician, Meowscarada very\noccasionally attempts to perform magic\nwhile it sleeps, but the performance\nnever seems to go as planned.
Fuecoco loves to eat and will dash with sparkling\neyes toward any food it sees. Sometimes it\nmoves its big mouth while it sleeps, as though\nchewing—perhaps it’s dreaming about eating a\ntasty treat.
It loves to sing and sometimes moves\nas though singing while it sleeps. Even when\nCrocalor’s body sways rhythmically in its sleep,\nthe egg-shaped fireball on the Pokémon’s\nhead never falls.
The fireball on its head has gained a soul, and when\nSkeledirge’s sleep is shallow, the fireball moves\naround and sometimes plays pranks. Skeledirge is\nan excellent caretaker and never gets angry no\nmatter what’s done to it. It just keeps sleeping.
Quaxly likes to keep things tidy, and\nthe gel it secretes repels grime. Sometimes\nwhen it sleeps for a long time, the coif on\nits head grows dry and messy, though\nthis is extremely rare.
It has a stoic temperament and often maintains its\nbeautiful posture as it sleeps. However, its daily\ntraining may sometimes leave it too tired to care for\nits coif, which is occasionally left messy as it sleeps.
Dancing in ways that evoke far-away places,\nQuaquaval mesmerizes all that see it. Even\nwhile asleep, it sways its whole body at every\nopportunity and is sometimes even seen striking\nelegant poses.
It has underdeveloped electric sacs on its cheeks.\nThese sacs can produce electricity only if Pawmi\nrubs them furiously with the pads on its forepaws.\nIt sometimes discharges electricity by rubbing its\ncheeks with its palms while sleeping—perhaps\nit’s practicing how to generate electricity.
Pawmo uses a unique fighting technique in which it\nuses its forepaws to strike foes and zap them with\nelectricity from its paw pads simultaneously.\nIt sometimes sleeps in its characteristic battle\nstance—perhaps it’s training its fighting moves in\nits dreams.
Pawmot has a peaceful personality and sometimes\nheals injured Pokémon by discharging electricity\nfrom its forepaws. If you see it raising its paws\nwhile it sleeps, it may be tending to other Pokémon\nin its dreams.
The hammer Tinkatink holds is made by hand\nthrough the pounding of scrap iron. Tinkatink is\nalso sometimes found holding it with care when\nit sleeps.
Tinkatuff is always walking around with a large\nhammer. The hammer isn’t just a tool for battle\nbut also seems to serve as a pillow whenever\nTinkatuff sleeps.
Tinkaton is intelligent and very daring. It is often\nseen sleeping with its body resting on top of its\ngigantic hammer. The hard metal may be a comfy\nplace to sleep for Tinkaton.
Cetoddle lives in cold regions, in small pods.\nSometimes it gets cranky and pounds the ground\nin its sleep, like it’s in a panic, possibly because\nit’s dreaming of being left behind by the group.
Not only can the horn on its upper jaw freeze its\nsurroundings, but it can also emit sound waves to\ncommunicate with others in its pod. On very rare\noccasions, it emits sound waves in its sleep,\npossibly unconsciously calling out to its friends.
Clodsire lives in ponds and swamps. It will carry\nWooper on its back and ferry them from one shore to\nthe other. On rare occasions, it can be seen sleeping\nin the mud; this may be because it got sleepy\nwhile ferrying Wooper.
This Pokémon prefers to sleep in freezing cold\nplaces. Sometimes if its resting spot gets too warm,\nit exhales air colder than −58 degrees Fahrenheit to\nbring its surroundings down to a more comfortable\ntemperature.
It lives on mountains perpetually covered in snow,\nand it can create ice particles in its coat. If you\nwatch carefully, you may see it sparkling in its sleep\nas it bathes the area around it with powdery snow.
It used to live in the water but now Wooper also live\non land. A poisonous film protects its body. It\nusually makes its home in muddy wetlands, so you\ncan sometimes see it sleeping with its whole body\ncovered in mud.
When this Pokémon generates electricity in\nits body, a sound like a rhythm played by a bass\nguitar reverberates all around. It sometimes\nseems as though it’s playing a musical instrument\nwhile it sleeps. Perhaps it’s dreaming of\nstanding on stage and performing.

Text File : MD_sleep_tips
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
When we go a number of days without\ngetting enough sleep, we accumulate\n“sleep debt.” Having sleep debt makes\nus less able to function when we’re\nawake, and what’s more, it poses\nvarious risks to our health.
Sleeping in longer on your days off\ndoes help relieve sleep debt, but you\ncan’t store up sleep in advance. If\nyou’re able to sleep for a long time, it\nprobably means you’ve accumulated\nsome sleep debt. It’s best to get good,\nregular sleep every day.
The best amount of sleep for you is\nhowever much it takes to not feel tired\nduring the day. Try to sleep the same\namount every day—even on days off. 
There are a very small number of people\nin the world who are known to be\nnaturally “short sleepers”—that is,\nthey can go without sleeping very much\nand still be healthy.
We’re either born as short sleepers or\nwe aren’t. At present, it’s just about\nimpossible for us to deliberately turn\nourselves into short sleepers.
When we’re short on sleep, sleeping\nshould be our top priority. Sleep debt\ncan make it difficult to enjoy our\ndaily activities and easier for us to\nget sick. It’s recommended to save\nwork and studies for later and focus\non getting enough sleep.
Ideally, we should go to bed at an hour\nwhen we can fall asleep easily, rather\nthan going out of our way to match\nsomeone else’s schedule.
People are born as early risers or night\nowls. It’s said to be extremely hard\nfor night people to deliberately become\nmorning people.
It’s best to eat dinner three to four\nhours before bed at the latest.\nDigesting our food thoroughly before\nsleep is important.
When our core body temperature\ndrops, we get sleepy. Eating makes our\ninternal organs start working, and that\nraises our core body temperature,\nmaking it harder to fall asleep.
We each run on our own biological\nclock, and its cycle isn’t exactly 24\nhours. Having breakfast after we wake\nup resets our biological clock, so we\ncan ideally sync up with the 24-hour\nday cycle.
We each run on our own biological\nclock, and its cycle isn’t an exact 24\nhours. Having breakfast or taking in\nsunlight after we wake up resets our\nbiological clock so we can sync up\nwith a 24-hour cycle.
If we sleep late on the weekends, we\nget what’s called “social jet lag.”\nThis makes it very difficult to get up\non Monday morning and also affects\nour health and performance.
It’s theorized that sleep lets our\nbrains recover from fatigue. We need\nsleep so our brains have time for\nregular upkeep.
Having a high core body temperature\nmakes it hard to sleep well. Taking a\nhot bath raises our core temperature,\nso a bath right before bed can make\nfalling asleep a challenge.
It’s recommended to finish up activities\nthat raise core body temperature—such\nas bathing and exercise—at least an\nhour before bed.
When we fall asleep, we first enter\nshallow non-REM sleep, then move on\nto deep non-REM sleep, and then to\nREM sleep, the stage when we have\nthe most vivid dreams. Repetitions of\nthese stages form our sleep cycles.
In one night’s sleep, we go through\nroughly four to six sleep cycles.
A single sleep cycle generally lasts\naround 90 minutes, but their lengths\ncan vary from person to person,\nday-to-day, or even within the same\nnight’s sleep.
Non-REM sleep is thought to be\nimportant for reinforcing and\nstrengthening memory and helping\nour bodies grow and recover from\nfatigue.
During REM sleep, our brains may be in\na state similar to being awake, even\nthough our bodies are asleep.
It was recently discovered that\nsome parts of our brains are more\nactive during REM sleep than when\nwe’re awake.
In order to get to sleep, we need to be\nsleepy enough—but what exactly is\nsleepiness? How does it work? No one\nhas a clear answer yet. Research into\nthe question is ongoing.
Some people find it hard to fall asleep\nwhen they’re hungry. It may be best\nto avoid eating dinner too early or too\nlate.
Caffeine affects the parts of our\nbrains that are responsible for making\nus sleepy. It may be good to avoid\ncaffeine starting five to six hours\nbefore you go to sleep.
It’s noted that our body temperature\nstarts to cool around the time we fall\nasleep and is lowest while we’re\nsleeping. This change in temperature\nis thought to be another important\nfactor for getting good sleep.
Our biological clocks are what make\nus wake up and start functioning when\nthe sun rises and get sleepy when\nnight falls.
Light is essential for regulating our\nbiological clocks. The sight of sunlight\nsoon after we wake up is what lets our\nbodies know it’s morning.
Our biological clocks are managed by a\n“master clock” in our brains. Seeing\nbright light at night throws off this\nmaster clock and interferes with sleep.\nIt’s good to keep our rooms as dark as\npossible when it’s time for bed.
It’s known that the more sleep we\nlose, the more prone we are to making\nmistakes. In other words, pulling an\nall-nighter to cram for a test can have\nthe opposite effect.
Getting enough sleep is very\nimportant for the healthy growth\nof our bodies and brains.
No one knows for sure why we\ndream, but some have suggested\nit has to do with the way our brains\nperform maintenance on themselves\nin our sleep.
How long we need to sleep depends on\nhow old we are. Kids need to sleep for\nlonger than adults do.
In our everyday lives, we tend to get\nless sleep than we should. First and\nforemost, you should try to prioritize\ngetting as much sleep as you need.
If we stay in the same position the\nwhole time we sleep, it can put strain\non our bodies. Rolling over helps\nlighten that strain.
When we sleep comfortably, we don’t\nroll over too often. If we sleep on\nbedding that’s too soft or too hard,\nwe roll over more often to reduce the\nstrain on our bodies.
Snoring a lot is known to make our\nsleep unstable. If you get the feeling\nyou haven’t been sleeping well, it may\nbe worth looking into how much you’ve\nbeen snoring.
The changing of the seasons seems to\nbe a factor in how long we sleep. It’s\nsaid that we sleep less when days are\nlonger and more when days are short.
Building up lots of sleep debt can be\ndangerous. It may cause us to\nexperience microsleeps—seconds-long\nlosses of consciousness—throughout\nthe day. To prevent this, it’s very\nimportant to sleep enough and in a\nconsistent way every day.
If you often find yourself suddenly\nfalling asleep during the day, it may\nbe a sign that you’ve built up lots of\nsleep debt. Please try your best to\nget enough sleep.
What stage of sleep we’re in is known\nto affect what kinds of dreams we\nhave. For example, research shows\nour dreams tend to be more vivid\nand clear when we’re experiencing\nREM sleep.
We’re said to be more creative after\na good night’s sleep, as opposed to\nnights we stay awake.
How much sleep we get may be linked\nto our bodies’ natural defenses. Some\nresearch suggests not getting enough\nsleep can make us more prone to\ncatching colds.
Being in too brightly lit places before\nbed can impact our sleep. Adjusting\nthe light in our surroundings may help\nbring healthier sleep.
If we don’t get enough sleep, we may\ndevelop more of a sweet tooth than\nusual. This is said to be because lack of\nsleep affects the part of our brains\nthat regulates food preferences.
If you find yourself tired during the\nday, taking a 15- to 20-minute nap\nmay help. But getting enough sleep at\nnight is best, so don’t forget to\nprioritize that.
If you have trouble falling asleep,\nit’s better to not go to bed when you\naren’t sleepy. It’s important to do\nsomething relaxing and wait for\nsleepiness to set in, rather than try\nto force sleep.
Research has shown that just warming\nup our feet before bed can help us\nsleep better. A foot bath before bed\nmay be worth a try.
Eating breakfast within an hour after\nwaking up is important for regulating\nour biological clocks. Even when we’re\nbusy, skipping breakfast and waiting\nuntil lunch isn’t the best idea.
Eating without chewing your food well\nis said to throw off your sleep cycle.\nThat means in order to sleep well, you\nneed to chew well, too.
Keeping your room as quiet as possible\nis one important factor in getting\ngood sleep. It’s said that sounds like\npeople talking can be especially\ndisruptive to sleep.
When you’re sleeping, try to maintain\nthe right temperature for you all the\nway until morning. Being too hot or\ntoo cold isn’t good for your sleep.
When going to sleep at night, it’s best\nto be in as complete darkness as\npossible. If you feel unsafe, using a\nnight light or other dim light is OK.
The memories managed while we sleep\ninclude body movements like the ones\nwe use for sports and playing\ninstruments. Getting good sleep after\npracticing soccer, basketball, or\npiano fingerwork might be a shortcut\nto improvement.
We know our bodies don’t move during\nREM sleep as our muscles don’t exert\nstrength. It’s thought this may be to\nprevent our bodies from moving\nintensely in reaction to our dreams.
We know that when we don’t get\nenough sleep, it’s much easier for us\nto get irritated or feel down. This may\nbe because lack of sleep makes it\ndifficult for our brains to properly\ncontrol our anger or doubts.
Memories of things we learned and\nexperienced during the day are\nmanaged during sleep so we can\nrecall them when we need to. Body\nmovements such as those used for\nsports or playing instruments may also\nimprove faster when we sleep well.
Our brains are active even while we’re\nexperiencing non-REM sleep, although\nin different ways than when we’re\nawake. Just because we’re sleeping\ndoesn’t mean our brains are resting.
It can be difficult to sleep peacefully\nwhen our stomachs are upset. Chewing\nthoroughly when we eat helps our\nstomach and intestines digest, and\nthat can lead to better sleep.
Short naps can go a long way in\npreventing afternoon sleepiness or\nsudden nodding off, and it’s said that\npeople who nap regularly may find\nthey’re able to do their best in their\nstudies or at work.
It’s said that naps of up to about 15\nminutes are good. Naps taken in the\nevening, or long naps, can affect a\nperson’s sleep at night, so exercise\ncaution.
In general, the time best suited for\nnaps is the time between when a\nperson eats lunch and around\n3:00 p.m. It’s at this time that a\nperson’s body rhythms make it easy\nto sleep.
A person doesn’t need to lie down on\ntheir side to take a nap, but it’s\nimportant to support one’s head on a\ndesk or with a cushion to prevent\none’s head from falling while trying to\nrest.
A short nap is no replacement for\nsleeping at night. Just because a nap\nprevents sleepiness, people should\ntake care not to depend too much on\nnaps.
Because we’re so busy every day, we\nsometimes try to make up for a lack of\nsleep by napping while riding in\nvehicles. Nodding off while being\nshaken on a train or bus does not equal\ndeep sleep. One must cherish proper\nsleep time in the comfort of a bed.

Notice of Update to Privacy Notice
About the “Review All at Once” Function
You can use the “Review All at Once” function\nwhen you sync multiple sleep data entries\nfrom another device, such as Pokémon GO\nPlus +. You can see the results of multiple sleep\nsessions together without going through sleep\nresearch for each one. Please note that if you\nsync three or more sleep data entries at the\nsame time, the “Review All at Once” function\nwill automatically be used on all entries except\nfor the most recent one.
[System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]All [System:Color a="255" ]sleep research will be [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]skipped[System:Color a="255" ].

View [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]unlimited[System:Color a="255" ] sleep data\nrecords and statistics
With a Regular Pass, you can only view your\ndata from the past 30 days. If you have a\nPremium Pass, you are able to review all\npast data without limit.
All past data is saved on the server,\nregardless of your current pass status,\nso you’ll get access to all your records\nwhenever you purchase a Premium Pass.
Benefit #1
Daily Bonus Biscuit upgraded\nto a [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]Premium Bonus Biscuit[System:Color a="255" ]
Instead of a regular Bonus Biscuit, you’ll\nget a Premium Bonus Biscuit each day\nat snack time.
A Premium Bonus Biscuit increases a\nPokémon’s friendship points by 4.
Benefit #2
Receive a bonus [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]100 sleep points\n[System:Color a="255" ]each day that you track sleep
Each day that you track your sleep, you\ncan get 100 extra sleep points.
These bonus sleep points will be awarded\njust once a day—so if you track more than\none sleep session in day, you will only get\nbonus sleep points for the first one.
Benefit #3
Gain access to the [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]Premium Exchange\n[System:Color a="255" ]for exchanging your sleep points
You can use the Premium Exchange when\nexchanging sleep points for items.
The item lineup in the Premium Exchange\nwill change monthly.
Benefit #4
Monthly gifts of a [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]Good Camp\nTicket [System:Color a="255" ]and [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]1,000 sleep points[System:Color a="255" ]
With an active Premium Pass, you can\nreceive gifts of a Good Camp Ticket and\n1,000 sleep points once per month.
Check the main Sleep Pass screen to see\nwhen your next monthly gifts will arrive.
Benefit #5
Additional [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]lavish rewards[System:Color a="255" ]\nevery three months
Every three months that you stay\nsubscribed to a Premium Pass, you’ll\nget a Handy Candy L, a Sub Skill Seed,\nand a Dream Cluster M.\n(The no-cost trial period does not count\ntoward your subscription streak.)
Tap the Loyalty Rewards button to see\nwhen your next quarterly reward is due.
Benefit #6
Unlock a [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]diary[System:Color a="255" ] where you can\nwrite notes on your sleep
You can write down all sorts of things in a\ndiary along with your sleep data. Try to jot\ndown observations and things you notice\nabout your sleep, how you felt when you\nwoke up, and more.
You can use up to 200 characters when\nwriting entries in your sleep diary.
Benefit #7

[System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]By having [System:Color a="255" ]Latias and Latios [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]on the same\nteam, the additional effect of their main\nskills will activate[System:Color a="255" ].
[System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]If you sleep alongside either one[System:Color a="255" ] (Latias or\nLatios) [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]for 150 hours, you can unlock the\nadditional effects of their main skills[System:Color a="255" ].
Latias and Latios
Send Your Pokémon to Nap Island
Pokémon that you send to Nap Island will\naccumulate [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]150 EXP a day[System:Color a="255" ], and when you\nget them back, they will gain the\naccumulated EXP. Send them as often as\nyou can!
Please keep in mind, though, that if you take\nPokémon back [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]before they’ve fully enjoyed\na seven-day stay[System:Color a="255" ] on Nap Island, [System:Color r="29" g="162" b="255" a="255" ]they will\nonly gain half of the EXP[System:Color a="255" ].
Note that a Pokémon’s Nature has the following effects\non EXP gains: Only Natures that increase EXP gains have\nan effect. Natures that decrease EXP gains won’t apply.

Drowsy Power Bonus
Add Cresselia to your team
If you [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]have a Cresselia on your team[System:Color a="255" ] when you\nsleep during the Cresselia vs. Darkrai event,\n[System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]Drowsy Power[System:Color a="255" ] will be [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]multiplied by 1.2[System:Color a="255" ].
Build Drowsy Power with Cresselia and\ncurtail Darkrai’s power!
Add Latias and Latios to your team
During the event, if you [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]have one of either\nLatias or Latios on your team[System:Color a="255" ] when you\nsleep, [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]Drowsy Power[System:Color a="255" ] will be [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]multiplied\nby 1.1[System:Color a="255" ].
During the event, if you [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]have both Latias\nand Latios on your team[System:Color a="255" ] when you sleep,\n[System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]Drowsy Power[System:Color a="255" ] will be [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]multiplied by 1.3[System:Color a="255" ].
Note: You can have both Latias and Latios on\nthe same team.
Build Drowsy Power with Latias and Latios\nto restore the Soul Dew’s brilliance!
Befriending Badges
Get Befriending Badges
If your friendship level with a Pokémon reaches\na certain level, you’ll get a [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]Befriending Badge[System:Color a="255" ].
Getting a badge will grant various bonuses,\nsuch as making it easier to encounter Shiny\nPokémon of that species or making sub skills\ngold.
The friendship level required to get Befriending\nBadges [System:Color r="255" g="107" b="55" a="255" ]differs for each Pokémon species[System:Color a="255" ]. You\ncan’t get Befriending Badges for Mythical Pokémon.
